\displaystyle\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\xi_{i}+\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle){\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|+\frac{1}{n}\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|.

Use Theorem
9
to bound the first term. Note that

{\mathbb{E}}\left[\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right]=0,

\|\Lambda_{\max}\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq\|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\|_{\Psi_{2}}\|\Lambda_{\max}\left({\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right)\|_{\Psi_{2}}=c\sqrt{d_{1}}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}},

{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)^{\top}\right]={\mathbf{M}}^{*}{\mathbf{M}}^{*\top}+d_{2}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}\cdot{\mathbf{I}}_{d_{1}},

{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)^{\top}\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)\right]={\mathbf{M}}^{*\top}{\mathbf{M}}^{*}+d_{1}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}\cdot{\mathbf{I}}_{d_{2}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle S^{2}:=

\displaystyle\max\left\{\Lambda_{\max}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)^{\top}\right]\right)\big/n,\right.

\displaystyle{~~~~~~~~}\left.\Lambda_{\max}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)^{\top}\left(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\right)\right]\right)\big/n\right\}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{1}^{2}+d_{1}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}.

Then, by Theorem
9
, we have

\displaystyle\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|

\displaystyle\leq c\sqrt{\sigma_{1}^{2}+d_{1}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log d_{1}}{n}}+c\frac{\log d_{1}}{n}\log\left(\frac{\sqrt{d_{1}}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{1}^{2}+d_{1}\|{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r\log d_{1}}{n}}\sigma_{1}

holds with probability over
1-c_{1}d_{1}^{-1}
.
Then consider the second term. Note that with probability over
1-c_{2}\exp(-d_{1})
, the following holds

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|\leq c_{3}\frac{\sqrt{d_{1}}}{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

(36)

Lemma
19
analyzes tail bound for sum of squared heavy-tailed random variables and proves with probability exceeding
1-(\log d_{1})^{-1}
, the following holds

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\leq(2n\gamma_{1}\log d_{1})^{\frac{2}{1+\epsilon}}

Instert it into Equation
36
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|\leq\tilde{c}_{3}\sqrt{d_{1}}n^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}\left(\log d_{1}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon}}\gamma_{1}^{\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|\leq c_{0}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r\log d_{1}}{n}}\sigma_{1}+\tilde{c}_{3}\sqrt{d_{1}}n^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}\left(\log d_{1}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon}}\gamma_{1}^{\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}},

which implies
\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|\leq\sigma_{r}/4
. Then by Lemma
16
, we have

\displaystyle\|\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|\leq c_{4}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r\log d_{1}}{n}}\sigma_{1}+c_{5}\sqrt{d_{1}}n^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}\left(\log d_{1}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon}}\gamma_{1}^{\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}}.

Note that
\text{rank}({\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})\leq 2r
. Then, it has

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2r}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2r}\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{4}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}r^{2}\log d_{1}}{n}}\sigma_{1}+\tilde{c}_{5}\sqrt{d_{1}r}n^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}}\left(\log d_{1}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon}}\gamma_{1}^{\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}},

which completes the proof.

Theorem 9
.

(Bernstein’s Inequality, Proposition 2 in
Koltchinskii et al. 2011
)
Let
{\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{A}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathbf{A}}_{n}
be i.i.d.
p\times q
matrices that satisfy for some
\alpha\geq 1
(and all
i
)

{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{A}}_{i}=0,\qquad\|\Lambda_{\max}({\mathbf{A}}_{i})\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}=:K<+\infty.

Define

S^{2}:=\max\left\{\Lambda_{\max}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{A}}_{i}{\mathbf{A}}_{i}^{\top}\right)\big/n,\Lambda_{\max}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbf{A}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}_{i}\right)\big/n\right\}.

Then for some constant
C>0
and for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\Lambda_{\max}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbf{A}}_{i}\right)\big/n\geq CS\sqrt{\frac{t+\log(p+q)}{n}}+CK\log^{1/\alpha}\left(\frac{K}{S}\right)\frac{t+\log(p+q)}{n}\right)\leq\exp(-t).

Appendix D
Proof of Tensor Regression Initialization Theorem
8

For any two orthogonal matrices
{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{V}}\in{\mathbb{O}}_{d,r}
, their distance with optimal rotation is defined to be
d({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{V}}):=\min_{{\mathbf{R}}\in{\mathbb{O}}_{r}}\|{\mathbf{U}}-{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{R}}\|_{\rm{F}}
. First estimate distance between
{\mathbf{U}}_{i}^{(0)}
and
{\mathbf{U}}_{i}^{*}
. Denote

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathbf{N}}}=\frac{1}{2n(n-1)}\sum_{1\leq i<i^{\prime}\leq n}^{n}\tilde{Y}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i^{\prime}}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i^{\prime}}^{T}+{\mathbf{X}}_{i^{\prime}}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{T}).

(37)

From Lemma
13
and Wedin’s sin
\Theta
theorem, we obtain that with probability exceeding
1-c_{0}m\bar{d}^{-10}
, for all
i\in[m]
,

\displaystyle d({\mathbf{U}}_{i}^{(0)},{\mathbf{U}}_{i}^{*})\leq\sqrt{2r_{i}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{N}}_{i}-{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{*}{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{*\top}\|/\underline{\lambda}^{2}\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\log\bar{d}}\frac{(d^{*})^{1/4}r_{i}}{n^{1/2}}\kappa^{2}+C_{2}\sqrt{\log\bar{d}}\frac{(d^{*})^{1/2}r_{i}^{1/2}}{n}\cdot\frac{\|\xi\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}.

(38)

Decompose
{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}
and we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\|{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}+m\bar{\lambda}\max_{j=1,\ldots,m}d({\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)},{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{*}).

(39)

We now bound
\|{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}
. Definition of
{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}
implies
\nabla_{{\mathbf{C}}}L_{n}({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}\times_{i}{\mathbf{U}}_{i}^{(0)})=0
. Then expand square of
\|{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}
.

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\langle\nabla_{{\mathbf{C}}}L({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)})-\nabla_{{\mathbf{C}}}L({\mathbf{C}}^{*}\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}),{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\langle(\nabla_{{\mathbf{C}}}L-\nabla_{{\mathbf{C}}}L_{n})({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}),{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\rangle}_{\beta_{1}}-\underbrace{\langle\nabla_{{\mathbf{C}}}L({\mathbf{C}}^{*}\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}),{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\rangle}_{\beta_{2}}.

(40)

The first term on the right hand side can be analyzed as follows:

\displaystyle\langle(\nabla_{{\mathbf{C}}}L-\nabla_{{\mathbf{C}}}L_{n})({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}),{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\rangle=\langle(\nabla L-\nabla L_{n})({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}),({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*})\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}\rangle

\displaystyle{~~~~~~~~~~~~~}=\langle(\nabla L-{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi}\nabla L_{n})({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}),({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*})\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}\rangle

\displaystyle{~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~}+\langle({\mathbb{E}}_{\xi}\nabla L_{n}-\nabla L_{n})({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}),({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*})\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}\rangle.

Notice that the first term is

\displaystyle{~~~~}\langle(\nabla L-{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi}\nabla L_{n})({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}),({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*})\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle\langle({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*})\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle-\langle{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*})\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}\rangle.

Using a standard empirical process argument, we get as long as
n\gtrsim\sum_{j}^{m}d_{j}r_{j}+r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}
, with probability exceeding
1-\exp(-C(\sum_{j}^{m}d_{j}r_{j}+r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}))
,

\sup_{{\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{B}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{{\mathbf{r}}},\|{\mathbf{A}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}},\|{\mathbf{B}}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq 1}|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{A}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle\langle{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle-\langle{\mathbf{A}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*},{\mathbf{B}}\rangle|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}d_{j}r_{j}+r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}}{n}},

by which the first term could be bounded with:

\begin{split}&~~~~|\langle(\nabla L-{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi}\nabla L_{n})({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}),({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*})\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}\rangle|\\
&\leq c_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}d_{j}r_{j}+r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}}{n}}\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\|{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.\end{split}

(41)

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\langle({\mathbb{E}}_{y}\nabla L_{n}-\nabla L_{n})({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\times_{j\in[d]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}),({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*})\times_{j\in[d]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)T}\rangle=\langle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i},({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*})\times_{j\in[d]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}\rangle.

(42)

First fix the noise term
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
.
Then with a standard covering argument, we have with probability exceeding
1-c_{1}^{\prime}\exp(-(\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}))
,

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbf{A}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{{\mathbf{r}}},\|{\mathbf{A}}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq 1}\langle\sum_{i}^{n}\xi_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{A}}\rangle\leq c_{1}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{m}d_{j}r_{j}+r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\right)^{1/2},

(43)

where
c_{1},c_{1}^{\prime}>0
are some constants. By Lemma
18
, we obtain upper bound of sum of heavy-tailed variables,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\geq 3n\|\xi\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}\right)\leq 1-c_{2}^{\prime}n^{-\min\{\varepsilon/2,1\}}.

(44)

Insert Equation
43
,
44
into Equation
42
and it leads to

\displaystyle|\langle({\mathbb{E}}_{y}\nabla L_{n}-\nabla L_{n})({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}),({\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*})\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{i}^{(0)T}\rangle|

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}d_{j}r_{j}+r_{1}\cdots r_{m}}{n}}\cdot\|\xi\|_{2+\varepsilon}\|{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.

(45)

with probability exceeding
1-c_{1}^{\prime}\exp(-(\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}))-c_{2}^{\prime}n^{-\min\{\varepsilon/2,1\}}
. Then use Equation
41
,
D
to bound the first term on the R.H.S. of Equation
D
:

\displaystyle|\beta_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}d_{j}r_{j}+r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}}{n}}(\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}+\|\xi\|_{2+\varepsilon})\|{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}d_{j}r_{j}+r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}}{n}}\bigg(\|{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}+m\bar{\lambda}\max_{j=1,\ldots,m}d({\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)},{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{*})+\|\xi\|_{2+\varepsilon}\bigg)\|{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.

(46)

Now we consider
\beta_{2}
. Notice that

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{C}}}L({\mathbf{C}}^{*}\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)})\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle=\|{\mathbf{C}}^{*}(\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)}-\times_{j\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{*})\times_{k\in[m]}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(0)\top}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq m\bar{\lambda}\max_{j=1,\ldots,m}d({\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)},{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{*}).

Therefore

\displaystyle|\beta_{2}|\leq\|\nabla_{{\mathbf{C}}}L({\mathbf{C}}^{*}\times_{i}{\mathbf{U}}_{i}^{(0)})\|_{\rm{F}}\|{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq m\bar{\lambda}\max_{j=1,\ldots,m}d({\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)},{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{*})\|{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.

(47)

Now from (
D
), (
D
) and (
47
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{C}}^{(0)}-{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}d_{j}r_{j}+r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}}{n}}\|\xi\|_{2+\varepsilon}+C_{3}m\bar{\lambda}\max_{j=1,\ldots,m}d({\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)},{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{*}).

Now we go back to (
39
), and we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}d_{j}r_{j}+r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}}{n}}\|\xi\|_{2+\varepsilon}+(C_{3}+1)m\bar{\lambda}\max_{j=1,\ldots,m}d({\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(0)},{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{*}).

Now from (
38
) and under the sample size condition and SNR condition, we verified for some contant
C_{4}>0
,

\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq C_{4}\underline{\lambda},

holds with probability over
1-c_{1}^{\prime}\exp(-(\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}))-c_{2}^{\prime}n^{-\min\{\varepsilon/2,1\}}-c_{3}^{\prime}m\bar{d}^{-10}
.

Lemma 13
.

Let
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
be a rank
r
matrix and suppose
d_{1}\leq d_{2}
. Suppose
\{{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
is a sequence of i.i.d. sensing matrices with i.i.d. standard normal entries and
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
is a sequence of i.i.d. noise and then observe

Y_{i}=\langle{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle+\xi_{i},\,i=1,\cdots,n.

Denote
\sigma_{1}=\|{\mathbf{M}}\|
and
d=d_{1}\vee d_{2}
.
Suppose the following condition is satisfied:

(C1)

Sample size satisfies:
n\geq\sqrt{rd_{1}d_{2}}\log d
.

(C2)

Exists constant
\varepsilon>0
, such that
{\mathbb{E}}|\xi_{1}|^{2+\varepsilon}<+\infty
. Denote
\|\xi_{1}\|_{2+\varepsilon}:=({\mathbb{E}}|\xi_{1}|^{2+\varepsilon})^{1/(2+\varepsilon)}
.

Truncate
Y_{i}
with
\tau=\frac{n^{1/2}}{(d_{1}d_{2})^{1/4}}(\sqrt{r}\sigma_{1}+\gamma)
,
\tilde{Y}_{i}=\text{sign}(Y_{i})(|Y_{i}|\vee\tau)
and define

\tilde{{\mathbf{N}}}:=\frac{1}{n(n-1)}\sum_{1\leq i\neq i^{\prime}\leq n}\tilde{Y}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i^{\prime}}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i^{\prime}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{X}}_{i^{\prime}}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}).

Then there exist some constants
c_{0},C_{1},C_{2}>0
, such that with probability exceeding
1-c_{0}d^{-10}
,

\|\tilde{\mathbf{N}}-{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{M}}^{T}\|\leq C_{1}\frac{(d_{1}d_{2})^{1/4}\sqrt{\log d}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{r}\sigma_{1}^{2}+C_{2}\frac{(d_{1}d_{2})^{1/2}\sqrt{\log d}}{n}\|\xi\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}.

Proof.

For convenience, let
Y,{\mathbf{X}}
be i.i.d. with
\{Y_{i},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
and
\tilde{Y}:=\text{sign}(Y)\cdot(|Y|\vee\tau)
.
Notice that
(2+\varepsilon)
moment of
Y
could be bounded with

\displaystyle\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}:=({\mathbb{E}}|Y|^{2+\varepsilon})^{1/(2+\varepsilon)}\leq\sqrt{2+\varepsilon}\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}+\|\xi\|_{2+\varepsilon}\leq c_{0}\sqrt{r}\sigma_{1}+\|\xi\|_{2+\varepsilon}.

(48)

Notice that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathbf{N}}-{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{M}}^{\top}\|\leq\|\tilde{{\mathbf{N}}}-{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\|+\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{M}}^{\top}\|.

(49)

By decoupling techniques
[
De la Pena and Giné 2012
]
, it has for any
t>0
,

{\mathbb{P}}(\|\tilde{{\mathbf{N}}}-{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\|\geq t)\leq 15{\mathbb{P}}(\|\hat{{\mathbf{N}}}-{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\|\geq 15t),

where
\hat{\mathbf{N}}=\frac{1}{2n(n-1)}\sum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}\tilde{Y}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{j}^{\prime}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{j}^{\prime T}+{\mathbf{X}}_{j}^{\prime}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{T})
and
\{({\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\prime},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n}
is an independent copy of
\{({\mathbf{X}}_{i},\tilde{Y}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
such that

Y_{i}^{\prime}=\langle{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\prime}\rangle+\xi_{i}^{\prime},\quad i=1,\cdots,n.

Denote

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}},\quad\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tilde{Y}_{j}^{\prime}{\mathbf{X}}_{j}^{\prime}-{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}.

Then the first term of Equation
49
could be expressed in the way of

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{N}}-{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{2(n-1)}\bigg(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}^{T}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}^{T}+(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2})\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}+{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2})^{\top}\bigg)

\displaystyle~~~~+\frac{1}{n-1}\bigg(\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{Y}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{\prime}({\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\prime T}+{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\prime}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{T}-2{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{\top})\bigg).

Step 1. Bound
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\|,\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}\|
.
The proof follows Bernstein’s inequality Theorem
9
. Note that for any
{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d_{1}-1}
,
{\mathbf{v}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d_{2}-1}
,
|{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{v}}|\leq\tau{\mathbb{E}}|{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{v}}|\leq 2\tau
, which implies

\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|\leq 2\tau.

Furthermore, we have for some constant
c_{2}>0
,

\big\|\|\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|\big\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq c_{2}\tau\sqrt{d}.

Then consider
\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{T}\|
:

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{T}\|

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\|\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{T}\|\leq({\mathbb{E}}|Y_{i}|^{2+\varepsilon})^{2/(2+\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{E}}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|^{2\cdot\frac{2+\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}})^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2+\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}d.

Then we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{E}}(\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i})(\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i})^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{T}\|\leq c_{2}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}d.

Similarly,
\|{\mathbb{E}}(\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i})^{\top}(\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i})\|
could be bounded with
c_{2}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}d
. Thus by Bernstein’s inequality Theorem
9
, the following equation holds with probability exceeding
1-c_{4}d^{-10}
,

\displaystyle\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}\|\leq c_{3}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{n}}+c_{3}\tau\sqrt{d}\sqrt{\log\frac{c^{\prime}\tau}{\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}}}\cdot\frac{\log d}{n},

which finishes bounding
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\|
. It’s similar to
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}\|
and with a union bound, the following equation holds

\displaystyle\max\{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\|,\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}\|\}\leq c_{3}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{n}}+c_{3}\tau\sqrt{d}\sqrt{\log\frac{c^{\prime}\tau}{\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}}}\cdot\frac{\log d}{n}

with probability exceeding
1-2c_{4}d^{-10}
.

Step 2. Bound
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{T}\|,\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{T}\|
.
WLOG, we only consider
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{T}\|
and for convenience denote
\tilde{\mathbf{M}}={\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}
. Recall that
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{T}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}
. The proof also follows Bernstein’s inequality Theorem
9
. First, note that

{\mathbb{E}}[(\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{\top}]=0,\quad\big\|\|(\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{\top}\|\big\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq c_{1}\tau\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|\sqrt{d_{1}},

which uses
\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|\leq 2\tau
.
Then consider
\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{T}\|
:

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{T}\|

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\|Y_{i}^{2}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{T}\|\leq({\mathbb{E}}|Y_{i}|^{2+\varepsilon})^{2/2+\varepsilon}({\mathbb{E}}\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}\|^{2\cdot\frac{2+\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}})^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2+\varepsilon}}.

With standard
\varepsilon
-net techniques (see
Vershynin 2018
), we have for any
t>0
,
\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}\|\leq c_{0}(\sqrt{d_{1}}+t)\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|
holds with probability at least
1-2\exp(-t^{2})
and
{\mathbb{E}}\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}\|\leq c_{0}^{\prime}\sqrt{d_{1}}\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|
. Then combined with
\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|=\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}\|\leq c_{1}^{\prime}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{T}\|\leq c_{2}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}\|{\mathbf{M}}\|^{2}d_{1}.

Thus we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{E}}((\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T})((\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T})^{T}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{T}\|\leq c_{2}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|^{2}d_{1}.

Similarly, it has

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{E}}((\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T})^{\top}((\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T})\|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{\top}\|\leq c_{2}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|^{2}d_{1}.

By Bernstein’s inequality Theorem
9
, with probability exceeding
1-2d^{-10}
, the following equation holds

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}\|\leq c_{3}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}\log d}{n}}+c_{3}\tau\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|\sqrt{d}_{1}\sqrt{\log\frac{\tau}{\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}}}\cdot\frac{\log d}{n}

Term
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}\|
could be bounded in a similar fashion. Take a union bound and then we get with probability exceeding
1-4d^{-10}
,

\begin{split}\max&\{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}\|,\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}\|\}\\
&\leq c_{3}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}d\log d}{n}}+c_{3}\tau\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|\sqrt{d}_{1}\sqrt{\log\frac{\tau}{\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}}}\cdot\frac{\log d}{n}\end{split}

(50)

Step 3. Bound
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}^{T}\|
.
We shall bound
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}^{T}\|
under event

{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}=\{\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}\|\leq c_{3}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{n}}+c_{3}\tau\sqrt{d}\sqrt{\log\frac{c^{\prime}\tau}{\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}}}\cdot\frac{\log d}{n}\},

which is verified to hold with probability over
1-4d^{-10}
in Step 1. Replace
\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}
with
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}
of
\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}\|
in Step 2 and follow Step 2 procedure. Then we get with probability exceeding
1-2d^{-10}
,

\begin{split}&\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}^{T}\|\leq c_{4}\bigg(\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}\log d}{n}}+\tau\sqrt{d_{1}}\sqrt{\log\frac{\tau}{\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}}}\cdot\frac{\log d}{n}\bigg)\\
&{~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~}\times\bigg(\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{n}}+\tau\sqrt{d}\sqrt{\log\frac{c^{\prime}\tau}{\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}}}\cdot\frac{\log d}{n}\bigg).\end{split}

(51)

Step 4. Bound
\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{Y}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{\prime}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\prime T}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}\|
.
Notice that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{Y}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{\prime}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\prime T}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{\prime}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\prime}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})^{T}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}.

Note that the second term
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(Y_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}
is analyzed in Step 2. Analysis of the first term also follows Bernstein’s inequality Theorem
9
. Notice that

{\mathbb{E}}[\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{\prime}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\prime}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})^{T}]=0,\quad\big\|\|\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{\prime}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\prime}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})^{T}\|\big\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq c_{2}\tau^{2}d,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{\prime}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\prime}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})^{T}(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{\prime}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\prime}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}){\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{T}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}2}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}\top}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq({\mathbb{E}}|\tilde{Y}_{i}|^{2+\varepsilon}|\tilde{Y}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}|^{2+\varepsilon})^{2/(2+\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{E}}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}\|^{2\cdot\frac{2+\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}})^{\varepsilon/(2+\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\|\tilde{Y}_{i}\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{4}d_{1}d_{2},

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2}(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{\prime}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\prime}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}){\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{T}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{\prime}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\prime}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})^{T}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}2}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq({\mathbb{E}}|\tilde{Y}_{i}|^{2+\varepsilon}|\tilde{Y}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}|^{2+\varepsilon})^{2/(2+\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{E}}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}\top}\|^{2\cdot\frac{2+\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}})^{\varepsilon/(2+\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\|Y_{i}\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{4}d_{1}d_{2}.

Then by Bernstein’s inequality Theorem
9
, we have with probability exceeding
1-2d^{-10}
,

\displaystyle\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{Y}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{\prime}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{\prime}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}})^{T}\|\leq c_{3}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}+c_{3}\tau^{2}d\sqrt{\log\frac{\tau^{2}d}{\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}}}}\cdot\frac{\log d}{n}.

(52)

Step 5. Bound
\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{M}}^{\top}\|
.
Notice that

\|{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{M}}^{T}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{T}\|\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|(\|{\mathbf{M}}\|+\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|)
. Furthermore, we have for any
{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d_{1}-1}
,
{\mathbf{v}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d_{2}-1}
,

\displaystyle|{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}({\mathbf{M}}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}){\mathbf{v}}|

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}|Y|\cdot 1_{|Y|>\tau}\cdot|{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}{\mathbf{v}}|

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}|Y|^{2}|{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}{\mathbf{v}}|

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{-1}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}\cdot({\mathbb{E}}|{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}{\mathbf{v}}|^{1+2/\varepsilon})^{\varepsilon/(2+\varepsilon)}\leq c_{1}\tau^{-1}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2},

where
c_{1}>0
is some constant and it implies
\|{\mathbf{M}}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|\leq c_{1}\tau^{-1}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}
. Then, we have

\|{\mathbf{M}}\|=\sigma_{1},\quad\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}\|+\|{\mathbf{M}}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|\leq\sigma_{1}+c_{1}\tau^{-1}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{M}}^{\top}-\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}^{\top}\|\leq c_{1}\tau^{-1}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}\left(2\sigma_{1}+c_{1}\tau^{-1}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}\right).

(53)

Step 6. Put everything together.
Combine Equation
49
with Equation
50
,
51
,
52
and
53
and then we get with probability exceeding
1-c_{0}d^{-10}
,

\displaystyle\quad\|\tilde{\mathbf{N}}-{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{M}}^{T}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{n-1}\bigg(\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{2}^{\top}\|+2\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\|\bigg)+\frac{1}{n-1}\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{Y}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{\prime}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}\top}-{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\|+\|{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\tilde{Y}{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{M}}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\bigg(\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}\log d}{n}}+\tau\sqrt{d_{1}}\sqrt{\log\frac{\tau}{\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}}}\cdot\frac{\log d}{n}\bigg)\bigg(\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{n}}+\tau\sqrt{d}\sqrt{\log\frac{c^{\prime}\tau}{\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}}}\cdot\frac{\log d}{n}\bigg)

\displaystyle~~~~+3\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}\log d}{n}}+3\tau\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|\sqrt{d}_{1}\sqrt{\log\frac{\tau}{\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|}}\cdot\frac{\log d}{n}+c_{1}\tau^{-1}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}\left(2\sigma_{1}+c_{1}\tau^{-1}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle~~~~+\frac{1}{n-1}\bigg(\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}+\tau^{2}d\sqrt{\frac{\tau^{2}d}{\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}}}}\cdot\frac{\log d}{n}\bigg).

Insert
\|\tilde{{\mathbf{M}}}\|\leq\sigma_{1}+c_{1}\tau^{-1}\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2}
,
\|Y\|_{2+\varepsilon}\leq c_{0}\sqrt{r}\sigma_{1}+\|\xi\|_{2+\varepsilon}
and take
\tau\asymp\sqrt{n}(c_{0}\sqrt{r}\sigma_{1}+\|\xi\|_{2+\varepsilon})\left(d_{1}d_{2}\right)^{-1/4}
. Also, with sample size
n\geq\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}}\log d
, we finally have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathbf{N}}-{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{M}}^{T}\|\leq C_{1}\frac{(d_{1}d_{2})^{1/4}\sqrt{\log d}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{r}\sigma_{1}^{2}+C_{2}\frac{(d_{1}d_{2})^{1/2}\sqrt{\log d}}{n}\|\xi\|_{2+\varepsilon}^{2},

where
C_{1},C_{2}>0
are some constants.
∎

Appendix E
Technical Lemmas

Lemma 14

(Partial Frobenius Norm, Lemma 28 of
Tong et al. 2021a
)
.

Define partial Frobenius norm of matrix
{\mathbf{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
to be

\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},r}:=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sigma_{i}^{2}({\mathbf{M}})}=\|{\rm SVD}_{r}({\mathbf{M}})\|_{\rm{F}}.

For any
{\mathbf{M}},\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
with
\operatorname{rank}(\hat{{\mathbf{M}}})\leq r
, one has

|\langle{\mathbf{M}},\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}\rangle|\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}.

(54)

For any
{\mathbf{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
,
{\mathbf{V}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times r}
, one has

\|{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{V}}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\|{\mathbf{V}}\|.

(55)

Lemma 15
.

(Frobenius norm of projected sub-gradient)
Let
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
be a matrix with rank at most
r
. Suppose
\mathbf{G}\in\partial f({\mathbf{M}})
is the sub-gradient at
{\mathbf{M}}
and
\mathbb{T}
is the tangent space for
{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathbf{r}}
at
{\mathbf{M}}
. Then we have

\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{G})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{2}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}.

Proof.

Notice that

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}=\|{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{T}{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\|({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{T}){\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{V}}^{T}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}.

Then by properties of partial Frobenius norm Lemma
55
, one has

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{T}{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F}}=\|{\mathbf{U}}^{T}{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}\|{\mathbf{U}}\|=\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r},

\displaystyle\|({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{T}){\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{V}}^{T}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r},

which implies
\|{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}\leq 2\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},r}^{2}
.
∎

Lemma 16
.

(Matrix Perturbation)
Suppose matrix
{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
has rank
r
and has singular value decomposition
{\mathbf{M}}^{*}=\mathbf{U}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}=\operatorname{diag}\{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\cdots,\sigma_{r}\}
and
\sigma_{1}\geq\sigma_{2}\geq\cdots\geq\sigma_{r}>0
. Then for any
\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
satisfying
\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}<\sigma_{r}/4
, with
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times r}
and
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{r}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}\times r}
the left and right singular vectors of
r
largest singular values, we have

\displaystyle\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\sigma_{r}}\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|,\quad\|\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\|\leq\frac{4}{\sigma_{r}}\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|,

\displaystyle\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{{\mathbf{M}}})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}}\|+20\frac{\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma_{r}},

\displaystyle\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{{\mathbf{M}}})-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+20\frac{\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sigma_{r}}.

Proof.

See Section
F
.
∎

Theorem 10
.

(Empirical process in matrix recovery)
Suppose
f(\mathbf{M})
is defined in Equation
2
and
\rho(\cdot)
is
\tilde{L}
-Lipschitz continuous. Then there exists constants
C,C_{1}>0
, such that

\big|(f(\mathbf{M})-f(\mathbf{M}^{*}))-(\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{M})-\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{M}^{*}))\big|\leq(C_{1}+C\tilde{L})\sqrt{nd_{1}r}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(56)

holds for all at most
r
rank matrix
{\mathbf{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
with probability over
1-\exp\left(-\frac{C^{2}d_{1}r}{3}\right)-3\exp\left(-\frac{n}{\log n}\right)
.

Proof.

See Section
G
.
∎

Corollary 1
.

Suppose
f(\mathbf{M})
is defined in Equation
2
and
\rho(\cdot)
is
\tilde{L}
-Lipschitz continuous. Then there exists constants
C,C_{1}>0
, such that

\big|(f(\mathbf{M}+{\mathbf{M}}_{1})-f(\mathbf{M}))-(\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{M}+{\mathbf{M}}_{1})-\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{M}))\big|\leq(C_{1}+C\tilde{L})\sqrt{nd_{1}r}\|\mathbf{M}_{1}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(57)

holds for all at most
r
rank matrix
{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{M}}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
with probability over
1-\exp\left(-\frac{C^{2}d_{1}r}{3}\right)-3\exp\left(-\frac{n}{\log n}\right)
.

Proof.

It is similar to proof of Theorem
10
.
∎

Corollary 2
.

Suppose conditions in Theorem
10
hold and let
{\mathbf{X}}_{1},\dots,{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
have i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
entries. For any at most rank
r
matrix
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
, There exist constants
c,c_{1}>0
, such that when
n\geq cd_{1}r
, the following inequality

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\pi}}n\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|\leq 2n\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}

holds with high probability over
1-\exp(-c_{1}d_{1}r)
.

Proof.

Take the absolute loss function in Theorem
10
, namely,
\rho(\cdot)=|\cdot|
and set
{\mathbf{M}}^{*}
to be zero and instert
{\mathbb{E}}|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}
(see Lemma
17
) into it.
∎

Lemma 17
.

(Expectation of absolute loss under Gaussian noise) Suppose the noise term follows Gaussian distribution
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
and take the absolute value loss function

f({\mathbf{M}})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|Y_{i}-\langle{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\rangle|,

and then we have

\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{M})]=n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}},\quad\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{M}^{*})]=n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma.

Proof.

Note that
f(\mathbf{M})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}\rangle|=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|
,
f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\xi_{i}|
.

First calculate the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}[[|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{A}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|]\lvert\mathbf{A}_{i}]
. Notice that conditioned on
\mathbf{A}_{i}
,
\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{A}_{i},\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{X}^{*}\rangle
is normally distributed with mean
-\langle\mathbf{A}_{i},\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{X}^{*}\rangle
and variance
\sigma^{2}
and then
|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{A}_{i},\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{X}^{*}\rangle|
has a folded normal distribution.

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}&[[|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|]\lvert\mathbf{X}_{i}]\\
&=\sigma\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\exp(-\frac{\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}})-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle[1-2\phi(\frac{\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle}{\sigma})],\end{split}

where
\phi(\cdot)
is the normal cumulative distribution function. For each
i=1,2,\cdots,n
, one has

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|]&=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[[|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|]\lvert\mathbf{X}_{i}]]\\
&=\sigma\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\mathbb{E}[\exp(-\frac{\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}})]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle[1-2\phi(\frac{\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle}{\sigma})]]\end{split}

and notice that
\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2})
and then after integration calculation it is

\mathbb{E}[|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}.

Specifically, when
{\mathbf{M}}={\mathbf{M}}^{*}
, it leads to
\mathbb{E}[|\xi|]=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma
.
Thus, we have

\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{M})]=n\mathbb{E}[|\xi_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|]=n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}},\quad\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{\mathbf{M}}^{*})]=n\mathbb{E}[|\xi|]=n\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma.

∎

Lemma 18
.

(Contraction of Heavy Tailed Random Variables)
Suppose
\xi,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\cdots,\xi_{n}
are i.i.d. random variables and there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that its
1+\varepsilon
moment is finite, that is
{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|^{1+\varepsilon}<+\infty
. Then
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\xi_{i}|\leq 3{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|
with probability exceeding
1-{}^{*}\!cn^{-\min\{\varepsilon,1\}}
. Here
{}^{*}\!c>0
is some constant which depends on
{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|,{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|^{1+\varepsilon}
.

Proof.

First consider when
0<\varepsilon<1
. Denote
\varphi_{i}:=|\xi_{i}|
and take truncation
\bar{\varphi}_{i}:=|\xi_{i}|\cdot 1_{\{|\xi_{i}|<n\}}=\varphi_{i}\cdot 1_{\varphi_{i}>n}
. For conveniece, denote
\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}:=\left({\mathbb{E}}|\xi|^{1+\varepsilon}\right)^{1/(1+\varepsilon)}
and introduce independent random variable
\varphi
which has same distribution as
\varphi_{1},\cdots,\varphi_{n}
and define
\bar{\varphi}:=\varphi\cdot 1_{\{\varphi<n\}}
. The original problem is equivalent to bound
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}
with its expectation.

Note that
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\varphi_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\varphi_{i}\right)
could be written into sum of three parts,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\varphi_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\varphi_{i}\right)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\bar{\varphi}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\bar{\varphi}_{i}\right)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\varphi_{i}-\bar{\varphi}_{i}\right)+({\mathbb{E}}\varphi-{\mathbb{E}}\bar{\varphi}).

Analyze the three parts respectively. Consider the first term, for any constant
s>0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\bar{\varphi}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\bar{\varphi}_{i}\right)|>s\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{ns^{2}}{\textrm{Var}}\,\left(\bar{\varphi}\right)\leq\frac{1}{ns^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\bar{\varphi}^{1+\varepsilon}\bar{\varphi}^{1-\varepsilon}\leq\frac{\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}}{ns^{2}}\cdot n^{1-\varepsilon}=\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}s^{-2}n^{-\varepsilon},

where the intermediate equation uses Markov inequality and the last inequality is because of
\bar{\varphi}<n
. Then consider the second term, the probability of
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{\varphi}_{i}\neq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{\varphi}_{i}\neq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n{\mathbb{P}}\left(\varphi>n\right)\leq n\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\varphi^{1+\varepsilon}}{n^{1+\varepsilon}}=\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon}.

The last term measures the expectation difference and could be bounded as followed,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\varphi_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\bar{\varphi}_{i}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\varphi_{i}\cdot 1_{\varphi_{i}>n}\leq{\mathbb{E}}\varphi.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\varphi_{i}|>s\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{\varphi}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\varphi_{i}|>s\right)+{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{\varphi}_{i}\neq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{\varphi}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\bar{\varphi}_{i}|>s-({\mathbb{E}}\varphi-{\mathbb{E}}\bar{\varphi})\right)+\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{\varphi}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\bar{\varphi}_{i}|>s-{\mathbb{E}}\varphi\right)+\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon},

where the last inequality uses
{\mathbb{E}}\varphi_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\bar{\varphi}_{i}\leq{\mathbb{E}}\varphi
. Take
s=2{\mathbb{E}}\varphi
and then it becomes

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\varphi_{i}|>2{\mathbb{E}}\varphi\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}\varphi_{i}|>{\mathbb{E}}\varphi\right)+\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}[{\mathbb{E}}\varphi]^{-2}n^{-\varepsilon}+\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon},

which proves
{\mathbb{P}}(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\xi_{i}|\geq 3{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|)\leq{}^{*}\!cn^{-\varepsilon}
. When
\varepsilon\geq 1
, we have
{\mathbb{P}}(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\xi_{i}|\geq 3{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|)\leq{}^{*}\!cn^{-1}
whose proof is simpler and hence skipped.
∎

Lemma 19
.

(Tail inequality for sum of squared heavy-tailed random variables)
Suppose
\xi,\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{n}
are i.i.d. random variables and there exists
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
such that its
1+\varepsilon
moment is finite, that is
{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|^{1+\varepsilon}<+\infty
. Denote
\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}=\left({\mathbb{E}}|\xi|^{1+\varepsilon}\right)^{1/(1+\varepsilon)}
. Then for any
s>0
, tail bound for sum of squares could be upper bounded

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}>s\right)\leq 2n\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}s^{-\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}}.

Proof.

For any
s>0
, it has
{\mathbb{P}}\left(\xi^{2}\geq s\right)\leq s^{-\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}}{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|^{1+\varepsilon}
which uses Markov’s inequality. Introduce truncated random variable
\varphi_{i}:=\xi_{i}^{2}1_{\{\xi_{i}^{2}<s\}}
. Note that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}
could be written into sum of two parts

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\xi_{i}^{2}-\varphi_{i}).

Hence, we analyze these two parts respectively. First consider probability of
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\neq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\neq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n{\mathbb{P}}\left(\xi^{2}>s\right)\leq n\frac{{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|^{1+\varepsilon}}{s^{\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}}}=n\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}s^{-\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}}.

Then bound tail for sum of
\varphi_{i}
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}>s\right)

\displaystyle\leq ns^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}\varphi=ns^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}\varphi^{\frac{1+\epsilon}{2}}\varphi^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}}\leq n\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}s^{-\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}}.

Finally, combine the above two equations, it has

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}>s\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}>s\right)+{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\neq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}\right)\leq 2n\|\xi\|_{1+\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}s^{-\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 20

(Partial Frobenius norm for tensor)
.

Suppose
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times\dots\times d_{m}}
is an
m
-th order tensor. Denote
{\mathbf{r}}=(r_{1},\cdots,r_{m})^{\top}
. The partial Frobenius norm of
{\mathbf{M}}
is defined to be

\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}:=\sup_{{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\in{\mathbb{O}}_{d_{1},r_{1}},\cdots,{\mathbf{U}}_{m}\in{\mathbb{O}}_{d_{m},r_{m}}}\|{\mathbf{M}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\dots\times_{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{m}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}},

Then for any tensor
\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times\cdots\times d_{m}}
with Tucker rank at most
{\mathbf{r}}
, one has

|\langle{\mathbf{M}},\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}\rangle|\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}.

Specifically, it implies
\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}=\sup_{\hat{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathbf{r}},\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq 1}|\langle{\mathbf{M}},\hat{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|
. Furthermore, for any
{\mathbf{V}}_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times r_{1}},\ldots,{\mathbf{V}}_{m}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{m}\times r_{m}}
, one has

\|{\mathbf{M}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\cdots\times_{m}{\mathbf{V}}_{m}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}\|{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\|\cdots\|{\mathbf{V}}_{m}\|.

Proof.

For matrix
\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}
, there exist core tensor
\hat{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{r_{1}\times\dots\times r_{m}}
and orthogonal matrices
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{j}\in{\mathbb{O}}_{d_{j},r_{j}}
for each
j
such that
\hat{\mathbf{M}}=\hat{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\times_{1}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\times_{2}\dots\times_{m}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{m}
. Note that
\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}=\|\hat{\mathbf{C}}\|_{\rm{F}}
. Then one has

\displaystyle|\langle{\mathbf{M}},\hat{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|

\displaystyle=|\langle{\mathbf{M}}\times_{1}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\dots\times_{m}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{m}^{\top},\hat{\mathbf{C}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}\times_{1}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\dots\times_{m}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{m}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}}\|\hat{\mathbf{C}}\|_{\rm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}.

Note that the equality holds when
\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}=\|{\mathbf{M}}\times_{1}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\dots\times_{m}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{m}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{C}}={\mathbf{M}}\times_{1}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\dots\times_{m}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{m}^{\top}/\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}
, which shows
\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}=\sup_{\hat{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathbf{r}},\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq 1}|\langle{\mathbf{M}},\hat{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|
. It is obvious that for any matrices
{\mathbf{V}}_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{j}\times r_{j}}
, one has

\|{\mathbf{M}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\cdots\times_{m}{\mathbf{V}}_{m}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}\|{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\|\cdots\|{\mathbf{V}}_{m}\|.

∎

Lemma 21
.

For any tensor
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}
, suppose
{\mathbf{G}}\in\partial f({\mathbf{M}})
is the subgradient at
{\mathbf{M}}
and
{\mathbb{T}}
is the tangent space for
{\mathbb{M}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}
at
{\mathbf{M}}
. Then we have

\|{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\sqrt{m+1}\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}.

Proof.

Let
{\mathbf{M}}={\mathbf{C}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\times_{2}\cdots\times_{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{m}
be its Tucker decomposition. Then we have

{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}}({\mathbf{G}})={\mathbf{G}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\cdots\times_{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{m}^{\top}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}{\mathbf{C}}\times_{j\in[m]\backslash i}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}\times_{i}\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i},

with
\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i}=\left({\mathbf{G}}\times_{j\in[m]\backslash i}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{\top}\times_{i}({\mathbf{I}}_{d_{i}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{i}{\mathbf{U}}_{i}^{\top})\right)_{(i)}\left({\mathbf{C}}_{(i)}\right)^{\dagger}
.
It implies

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}}({\mathbf{G}})\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|{\mathbf{G}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\cdots\times_{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{m}^{\top}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\|{\mathbf{C}}\times_{j\in[m]\backslash i}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}\times_{i}\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(m+1)\|{\mathbf{G}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}^{2}.

∎

Lemma 22

(Lemma 3.2 of
Koch and Lubich 2010
)
.

For any
{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}
with
\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\frac{1}{16m(m+3)}\underline{\lambda}
, denote
{\mathbb{T}}
as the tangent space for
{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathbf{r}}
at
{\mathbf{M}}
and then we have

\|{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathbb{T}}}^{\perp}({\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*})\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\frac{8m(m+3)}{\underline{\lambda}}\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}.

Theorem 11
.

(Empirical process in tensor recovery)
Suppose
f(\cdot)
is defined in Equation
8
and
\rho(\cdot)
is
\tilde{L}
-Lipschitz continuous. Then there exist constants
C,C_{1}>0
, such that

\big|(f(\mathbf{M})-f(\mathbf{M}^{*}))-(\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{M})-\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{M}^{*}))\big|\leq(C_{1}+C\tilde{L})\sqrt{n(2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+2^{m}\cdot r_{1}\cdots r_{d})}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(58)

holds for all at most Tucker rank
{\mathbf{r}}
tensor
{\mathbf{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times\cdots\times d_{m}}
with probability over
1-3\exp\left(-\frac{n}{\log n}\right)-\exp\left(-C(2^{m}\cdot r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}+2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j})\right)
.

Proof.

See Section
H
.
∎

Corollary 3
.

Suppose
f(\cdot)
is defined in Equation
8
and
\rho(\cdot)
is
\tilde{L}
-Lipschitz continuous. Then there exist constants
C,C_{1}>0
, such that

\big|(f(\mathbf{M}+{\mathbf{M}}_{1})-f(\mathbf{M}))-(\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{M}+{\mathbf{M}}_{1})-\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{M}))\big|\leq(C_{1}+C\tilde{L})\sqrt{n(2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+2^{m}\cdot r_{1}\cdots r_{d})}\|\mathbf{M}_{1}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(59)

holds for all at most Tucker rank
{\mathbf{r}}
tensor
{\mathbf{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times\cdots\times d_{m}}
and all at most Tucker rank
2{\mathbf{r}}
tensor
{\mathbf{M}}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times\cdots\times d_{m}}
, with probability over
1-3\exp\left(-\frac{n}{\log n}\right)-\exp\left(-C(2^{m}\cdot r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}+2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j})\right)
.

Proof.

It is similar to proof of Theorem
11
.
∎

Corollary 4
.

Suppose conditions of Theorem
11
hold and let
{\mathbf{X}}_{1},\dots,{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
have i.i.d.
N(0,1)
entries. For any at most rank
{\mathbf{r}}
tensors
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{2{\mathbf{r}}}
, there exist constants
c,c_{1}>0
, such that when
n\geq c\sqrt{2^{m}\cdot r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}+2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}}
, the following inequality

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\pi}}n\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|\leq 2n\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}

holds with high probability over
1-\exp(-c_{1}(2^{m}\cdot r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}+2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}))
.

Proof.

Take the absolute loss function in Theorem
11
, namely,
\rho(\cdot)=|\cdot|
and instert
{\mathbb{E}}|\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}
(see Lemma
17
) into it.
∎

Lemma 23

(Partial Frobenius norm of random Gaussian Tensor)
.

Suppose
{\mathbf{X}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times\dots\times d_{m}}
has independent
N(0,1)
entries. Then for rank vector
{\mathbf{r}}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{m})^{\top}
, it has

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\|{\mathbf{X}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}\geq 2c\left(t+\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{d}r_{j}d_{j}+r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{d}}\right)\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-t^{2}\right)

Proof.

The proof uses
\varepsilon
-net arguments. Recall that
{\mathbb{M}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}=\{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times\dots\times d_{m}}:\text{rank}({\mathbf{M}}_{(j)})\leq r_{j},\,j=1,\cdots,m\}
is the set of tensors with Tucker rank at most
{\mathbf{r}}
. From definition of partial Frobenius norm, it has

\|{\mathbf{X}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}=\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathbf{r}},\|{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq 1}|\langle{\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|

and suppose it achieves the supremum at
{\mathbf{M}}_{0}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}
, namely,
\|{\mathbf{M}}_{0}\|_{\rm{F}}=1
and
\|{\mathbf{X}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}=|\langle{\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{M}}_{0}\rangle|
. Then there exist a core tensor
{\mathbf{C}}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{m}}
and orthogonal matrices
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{0}\in{\mathbb{O}}_{d_{1},r_{1}},\cdots,{\mathbf{U}}_{m}^{0}\in{\mathbb{O}}_{d_{m},r_{m}}
such that

{\mathbf{M}}_{0}={\mathbf{C}}_{0}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{0}\times_{2}\cdots\times_{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{m}^{0}.

Notice that
\|{\mathbf{C}}_{0}\|_{\rm{F}}=1
. Define
{\mathbb{F}}_{\mathbf{r}}=\{{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{r_{1}\times\dots\times r_{m}}:\|{\mathbf{C}}\|_{\rm{F}}=1\}
to be the set of tensors with unit Frobenius norm. Then it has one
\varepsilon/(m+1)
-net
{\mathcal{N}}_{\varepsilon/(m+1)}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\mathbf{r}}}
with cardinality
|{\mathcal{N}}_{\varepsilon/(m+1)}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\mathbf{r}}}|\leq(3(m+1)/\varepsilon)^{r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}}
respect to the Frobenius norm. Suppose
{\mathcal{N}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathcal{N}}_{m}
are
\varepsilon/(m+1)
-nets of orthogonal matrix sets
{\mathbb{O}}_{d_{1},r_{1}},\cdots,{\mathbb{O}}_{d_{m},r_{m}}
respectively with cardinalities

|{\mathcal{N}}_{1}|\leq(3(m+1)/\varepsilon)^{d_{1}r_{1}},\cdots,|{\mathcal{N}}_{m}|\leq(3(m+1)/\varepsilon)^{d_{m}r_{m}}.

See
Rauhut et al. 2017
,
Vershynin 2018
for more about
\varepsilon
-nets.
Hence, for tensor
{\mathbf{M}}_{0}={\mathbf{C}}_{0}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{0}\times_{2}\cdots\times_{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{m}^{0}
, there is close approximation in the nets. Exist
{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\varepsilon/(m+1)}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\mathbf{r}}},\,{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathbf{U}}_{m}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{m}
such that

\|{\mathbf{C}}_{0}-{\mathbf{C}}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\varepsilon/(m+1),\,\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{0}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\varepsilon/(m+1),\cdots,\|{\mathbf{U}}_{m}^{0}-{\mathbf{U}}_{m}\|_{\rm{F}}\leq\varepsilon/(m+1).

Denote the product as
{\mathbf{M}}={\mathbf{C}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\times_{2}\cdots\times_{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{m}
. Furthermore, net
{\mathcal{N}}:=\{{\mathbf{M}}={\mathbf{C}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\times_{2}\cdots\times_{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{m}:{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{\varepsilon/(m+1)}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\mathbf{r}}},\,{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{1},\dots,{\mathbf{U}}_{m}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{m}\}
forms an
\varepsilon
-net of
{\mathbb{M}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}
with cardinality
|{\mathcal{N}}|\leq(3(m+1)/\varepsilon)^{r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}}
.

Then come back to partial Frobenius norm of
{\mathbf{X}}
and we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{X}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}

\displaystyle=|\langle{\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{M}}_{0}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq|\langle{\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|+|\langle{\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{M}}_{0}-{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathcal{N}}}|\langle{\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|+\varepsilon\|{\mathbf{X}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}},

which implies

\|{\mathbf{X}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}\leq\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathcal{N}}}|\langle{\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|.

On the other hand, for any fixed
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathcal{N}}
, we have
\|\langle{\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle\|_{\Psi_{2}}^{2}=1
.
Then the inner product between
{\mathbf{X}}
and fixed tensor
{\mathbf{M}}
has the following tail bound

{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\langle{\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|\geq u\right)\leq 2\exp(-u^{2}).

Thus the union yields

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathcal{N}}}|\langle{\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|\geq u\right)\leq 2|{\mathcal{N}}|\exp(-u^{2})\leq(3(m+1)/\varepsilon)^{r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}}\exp(-u^{2}).

Take
\varepsilon=1/2
and
u=c(\sqrt{r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}}+t)
and it leads to

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathcal{N}}}|\langle{\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle|\geq u\right)\leq 2\exp(-t^{2}),

which proves

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\|{\mathbf{X}}\|_{\rm{F},{\mathbf{r}}}\geq 2u\right)\leq 2\exp(-t^{2}).

∎

Appendix F
Proof of Matrix Perturbation Lemma
16

To prove Lemma
16
, first consider simpler case when the matrix and the perturbation are symmetric.

Lemma 24
.

(Symmetric Matrix Perturbation)
Suppose symmetric matrix
\mathbf{M}^{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
has rank
r
. Let
\mathbf{M}^{*}=\mathbf{U}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\mathbf{U}^{\top}
be its singular value decomposition with
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}=\operatorname{diag}\{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\cdots,\lambda_{r}\}
,
|\lambda_{1}|\geq|\lambda_{2}|\geq\cdots\geq|\lambda_{r}|>0
. Then for any matrix
\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
satisfying
\|\hat{{\mathbf{M}}}-{\mathbf{M}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}<\sigma_{r}/4
with
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
eigenvectors of
r
largest absolute eigenvalues of
\hat{\mathbf{M}}
, then we have

\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|\leq\frac{4\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|},

\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|\leq\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|+20\frac{\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|^{2}}{\lambda_{r}},

\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+20\frac{\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\lambda_{r}}.

Proof.

Denote
\mathbf{Z}=\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}
. Define
\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times(d-r)}
such that
[\mathbf{U},\mathbf{U}_{\perp}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
is orthonormal and then define the projector

\mathfrak{P}^{\perp}:=\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top},\quad\mathfrak{P}^{-1}:=\mathbf{U}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{-1}\mathbf{U}^{\top}.

Write
\mathfrak{P}^{-k}=\mathbf{U}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{-k}\mathbf{U}^{\top}
, for all
k\geq 1
and for convenience when
k=0
, we write
\mathfrak{P}^{0}=\mathfrak{P}^{-1}
. Define the
k
-th order perturbation

\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z}):=\sum_{\mathbf{s}:s_{1}+\cdots+s_{k+1}=k}(-1)^{1+\tau(\mathbf{s})}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{1}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{2}}\cdots\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k+1}},

where
s_{1},\cdots,s_{k}
are non-negative integers and
\tau(\mathbf{s})=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{I}(s_{i}>0)
is the number of positive indices in
\mathbf{s}
. Work
Xia 2021
proves

\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}=\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z}).

Then norm of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})
coulded be bounded in the way of

\|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\|\leq\binom{2k}{k}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}\|^{k}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{k}}\leq\left(\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}\right)^{k},\quad\|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\binom{2k}{k}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{k}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{k}}\leq\left(\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{|\lambda_{r}|}\right)^{k}.

Hence, the above three equations lead to

\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}\|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\|\leq\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|},\quad\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}\|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{|\lambda_{r}|}.

Then consider
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}^{*}
:

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}^{*}&=\sum_{\mathbf{s}:s_{1}+\cdots+s_{k+1}=k}(-1)^{1+\tau(\mathbf{s})}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{1}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{2}}\cdots\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k+1}}\mathbf{M}^{*}\\
&=\sum_{\mathbf{s}:s_{1}+\cdots+s_{k+1}=k}(-1)^{1+\tau(\mathbf{s})}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{1}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{2}}\cdots\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k+1}}\mathbf{U}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\mathbf{U}^{\top}.\end{split}

Note that when
s_{k+1}=0
,
\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k+1}}=\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}
and hence
\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k+1}}\mathbf{M}^{*}=0
and thus we have

\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}^{*}=\sum_{\mathbf{s}:s_{1}+\cdots+s_{k+1}=k,\,s_{k+1}>0}(-1)^{1+\tau(\mathbf{s})}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{1}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{2}}\cdots\mathfrak{P}^{-s_{k}}\mathbf{Z}\mathfrak{P}^{1-s_{k+1}}.

Specifically, when
k=1
, it is
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},1}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}^{*}=\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}
and its operator norm could be bounded with

\|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}^{*}\|\leq\binom{2k-1}{k}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}\|^{k+1}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{k-1}}\leq\|\mathbf{Z}\|\left(\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}\right)^{k-1},

which implies

\|\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}^{*}\|\leq\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|}.

Similarly, Frobenius norm of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}^{*}
has

\|\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\binom{2k-1}{k}\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}\|^{k}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{k-1}}\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left(\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}\right)^{k-1},

\|\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}.

Then expand
\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}^{*}
:

\begin{split}&{~~~~}\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}^{*}\\
&=\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}^{*}+\mathbf{Z})\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\\
&=\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}(\mathbf{M}^{*}+\mathbf{Z})\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{M}^{*}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\\
&=(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})\mathbf{M}^{*}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{M}^{*}(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})+\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}\\
&=\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}^{*}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{M}^{*}\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})+\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\\
&{~~~}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}\\
&=\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}^{*}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{M}^{*}\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})+\mathbf{Z}-\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\\
&{~~~}+(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}+(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})\\
\end{split}

Take Frobenius norm on each side of the above equation and it leads to

\begin{split}&{~~~~}\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\\
&\leq\|\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\mathbf{M}^{*}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{M}^{*}\sum_{k\geq 2}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{M}^{*},k}(\mathbf{Z})\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|\mathbf{Z}-\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\\
&{~~~}+\|(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\\
&\leq\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}+\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}+\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+3\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\\
&\leq\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+20\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}.\end{split}

Similarly, one has the bound of the operator norm

\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|\leq\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|+20\frac{\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|^{2}}{\lambda_{r}}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
16
.

Here we construct symmetrization of
\mathbf{M}^{*}
and
\hat{\mathbf{M}}
and then we could apply Lemma
24
. First construct symmetric matrices

\mathbf{Y}^{*}:=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\mathbf{M}^{*}\\
\mathbf{M}^{*\top}&0\end{matrix}\right),\,\hat{\mathbf{Y}}:=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\hat{\mathbf{M}}\\
\hat{\mathbf{M}}^{\top}&0\end{matrix}\right).

Denote the perturbation matrix as
\mathbf{Z}:=\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}^{*}
and similarly define

\hat{\mathbf{Z}}:=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\mathbf{Z}\\
\mathbf{Z}^{\top}&0\end{matrix}\right)=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\\
\hat{\mathbf{M}}^{\top}-\mathbf{M}^{*\top}&0\end{matrix}\right).

Note that
\operatorname{rank}(\mathbf{Y}^{*})\leq 2r
.
By Lemma
24
, one has

\|\operatorname{SVD}_{2r}(\hat{\mathbf{Y}})-\mathbf{Y}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|\hat{\mathbf{Z}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+20\frac{\|\hat{\mathbf{Z}}\|\|\hat{\mathbf{Z}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{|\lambda_{2r}(\mathbf{Y}^{*})|}

(60)

Suppose
{\mathbf{M}}^{*}
has singular value decomposition
\mathbf{M}^{*}=\mathbf{U}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
, where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}=\operatorname{diag}(\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{r})\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
with
\sigma_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\sigma_{r}>0
. Notice that

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\mathbf{U}&\mathbf{U}\\
\mathbf{V}&-\mathbf{V}\end{matrix}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d\times 2r}

is an orthogonal matrix and its columns contain
2r
linearly independent eigenvectors of
\mathbf{Y}^{*}

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\mathbf{U}&\mathbf{U}\\
\mathbf{V}&-\mathbf{V}\end{matrix}\right)^{\top}\mathbf{Y}^{*}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\mathbf{U}&\mathbf{U}\\
\mathbf{V}&-\mathbf{V}\end{matrix}\right)=\left(\begin{matrix}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}&0\\
0&-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\end{matrix}\right)

Thus, we have
|\lambda_{2r}(\mathbf{Y}^{*})|=\sigma_{r}
. Similarly, we could prove

\operatorname{SVD}_{2r}(\hat{\mathbf{Y}})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}&\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\\
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{r}&-\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{r}\end{matrix}\right)^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{Y}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}&\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\\
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{r}&-\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{r}\end{matrix}\right)=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})\\
\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}}^{\top})&0\end{matrix}\right),

which implies

\|\operatorname{SVD}_{2r}(\hat{\mathbf{Y}})-\mathbf{Y}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\sqrt{2}\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

Combine the above equation with
\|\hat{\mathbf{Z}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\sqrt{2}\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
,

\|\hat{\mathbf{Z}}\|=\|\mathbf{Z}\|
and Equation
60
becomes

\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+20\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}\|\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sigma_{r}}.

Notice that proof of
\|\operatorname{SVD}_{r}(\hat{\mathbf{M}})-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+20\frac{\|\mathbf{Z}\|\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\sigma_{r}}
is similar to the Frobinius norm case. Apply the perturbated eigenvector results of Lemma
24
here and it becomes

\bigg\|\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}&\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\\
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{r}&-\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{r}\end{matrix}\right)^{\top}\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}&\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\\
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{r}&-\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{r}\end{matrix}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{matrix}\mathbf{U}&\mathbf{U}\\
\mathbf{V}&-\mathbf{V}\end{matrix}\right)^{\top}\left(\begin{matrix}\mathbf{U}&\mathbf{U}\\
\mathbf{V}&-\mathbf{V}\end{matrix}\right)\bigg\|\leq\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{\sigma_{r}},

(61)

which implies

\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|\leq\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{\sigma_{r}}=\frac{4}{\sigma_{r}}\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|.

(62)

\|\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{r}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{r}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\|\leq\frac{4\|\mathbf{Z}\|}{\sigma_{r}}=\frac{4}{\sigma_{r}}\|\hat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{M}\|.

(63)

∎

Appendix G
Proof of Matrix Recovery Empirical Process Theorem
10

Proof.

The proof follows from Theorem
14
. Denote
{\mathbb{M}}_{r}:=\{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}:\text{rank}({\mathbf{M}})\leq r\}
the set of matrices with rank bounded by
r
. And consider

\displaystyle~~~~Z:=\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}}|f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})-{\mathbb{E}}(f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*}))|/\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.

Together with
f({\mathbf{M}})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle-y_{i})
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}Z

\displaystyle\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}\cdot\big(\rho(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}\rangle-y_{i})-\rho(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle-y_{i})\big)\right|\cdot\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq 4\tilde{L}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}\cdot\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\right|\cdot\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq 4\tilde{L}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|_{\mathrm{F,2r}}\leq 4\tilde{L}\cdot\sqrt{2r}{\mathbb{E}}\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|,

where
\{\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
is Rademacher sequence independent of
{\mathbf{X}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
. The first inequality is from Theorem
12
and the second inequality is from Theorem
13
. Notice that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}
has i.i.d.
N(0,n)
entries and therefore

{\mathbb{E}}\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|_{2}\leq C_{1}\sqrt{nd}
for some absolute constant
C_{1}>0
. And thus we have

{\mathbb{E}}Z\leq 4\sqrt{2}C_{1}\tilde{L}\sqrt{nd_{1}r}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle~~~~\|\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}}|\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}\rangle)-\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle)|\cdot\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}\|_{\Psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{L}\|\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}}|\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|\cdot\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}\|_{\Psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{L}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|_{\mathrm{F,2r}}\leq C_{2}\tilde{L}\sqrt{d_{1}r},

for some absolute constant
C_{2}>0
.
Then, by Lemma
25
, the Orlcz norm of its maximum in
i=1,\cdots,n
could be bounded:

\displaystyle\|\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}}|\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}\rangle)-\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle)|\cdot\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq C_{2}\tilde{L}\sqrt{d_{1}r\log n},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{r}}|\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}\rangle)-\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle)|\cdot\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}\right]\leq 3C_{2}\tilde{L}\sqrt{d_{1}r\log n}.

Also, note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}\rangle)-\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle)\big)^{2}\big]/\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|^{2}\leq\tilde{L}^{2}\mathbb{E}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle^{2}/\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|^{2}=\tilde{L}^{2}.

Invoke Theorem
14
and take
\eta=1
,
\delta=0.5
and there exists some constant
C>0
, and we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Z

\displaystyle\geq 8\sqrt{2}C_{2}\sqrt{d_{1}r}+t)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{3n\tilde{L}^{2}}\right)+3\exp\left(-\left(\frac{t}{C\tilde{L}\sqrt{d_{1}r\log n}}\right)^{2}\right)

holds for any
t>0
.
Take
t=C\sqrt{nd_{1}r}\tilde{L}
and then we have

\mathbb{P}(Z\geq 8\sqrt{2}C_{1}\sqrt{nd_{1}r}+C\tilde{L}\sqrt{nd_{1}r})\leq\exp\left(-\frac{C^{2}d_{1}r}{3}\right)+3\exp\left(-\frac{n}{\log n}\right),

which completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 12

(Symmetrization of Expectations,
[
Van Der Vaart et al. 1996
]
)
.

Consider
\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
independent matrices in
\chi
and let
\mathcal{F}
be a class of real-valued functions on
\chi
. Let
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{n}
be a Rademacher sequence independent of
\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
, then

\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f(\mathbf{X}_{i})-\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{X}_{i}))\big|\big]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}f(\mathbf{X}_{i})\big|\big]

(64)

Theorem 13

(Contraction Theorem,
[
Ludoux and Talagrand 1991
]
)
.

Consider the non-random elements
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
of
\chi
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a class of real-valued functions on
\chi
. Consider the Lipschitz continuous functions
\rho_{i}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with Lipschitz constant
L
, i.e.

\left|\rho_{i}(\mu)-\rho_{i}(\tilde{\mu})\right|\leq L|\mu-\tilde{\mu}|,\text{ for all }\mu,\tilde{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}

Let
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{n}
be a Rademacher sequence
.
Then for any function
f^{*}:\chi\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}\left\{\rho_{i}\left(f\left(x_{i}\right)\right)-\rho_{i}\left(f^{*}\left(x_{i}\right)\right)\right\}\right|\right]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[L\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mid\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}\left(f\left(x_{i}\right)-f^{*}\left(x_{i}\right)\right)\mid\right]

(65)

Theorem 14

(Tail inequality for suprema of empirical process
[
Adamczak 2008
]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
be independent random variables with values in a measurable space
(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{B})
and let
\mathcal{F}
be a countable class of measurable functions
f:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
. Assume that for every
f\in\mathcal{F}
and every
i,\mathbb{E}f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)=0
and for some
\alpha\in(0,1]
and all
i,\left\|\sup_{\mathcal{F}}\left|f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)\right|\right\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}<\infty.
Let

Z=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)\right|

Define moreover

\sigma^{2}=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}

Then, for all
0<\eta<1
and
\delta>0
, there exists a constant
C=C(\alpha,\eta,\delta)
, such that for all
t\geq 0

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(Z&\geq(1+\eta)\mathbb{E}Z+t)\\
&\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2(1+\delta)\sigma^{2}}\right)+3\exp\left(-\left(\frac{t}{C\left\|\max_{i}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)\right|\right\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}}\right)^{\alpha}\right)\end{split}

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Z

\displaystyle\leq(1-\eta)\mathbb{E}Z-t)
