\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2(1+\delta)\sigma^{2}}\right)+3\exp\left(-\left(\frac{t}{C\left\|\max_{i}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)\right|\right\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}}\right)^{\alpha}\right)

Remark 2
.

Notice that here we require
\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{X}_{i})=0
and when
\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{X}_{i})\neq 0
,
\mathbf{Z}
should be

Z=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)-\mathbb{E}f(\mathbf{X}_{i})\right|,

and tail inequality of
Z
would be

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(Z&\geq(1+\eta)\mathbb{E}Z+t)\\
&\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2(1+\delta)\sigma^{2}}\right)+3\exp\left(-\left(\frac{t}{C(\left\|\max_{i}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|f\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)\right|\right\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}+\mathbb{E}[\max_{i}|f(\mathbf{X}_{i})|])}\right)^{\alpha}\right)\end{split}

The following lemma is upper bound of Orlicz norm of maximum for
n
random variables.

Lemma 25
.

(Maximum of Sub-Gaussian)
Let
X_{1},\cdots,X_{n}
be a sequence of centered sub-gaussian random variables. Then the expectation and the Orlicz norm of the maximum could be upper bounded

\mathbb{E}[\max_{i}X_{i}]\leq K\sqrt{2\log n},

\mathbb{P}(\max_{i}X_{i}>t)\leq n\exp(-\frac{t^{2}}{2K^{2}}),

where
K=\max_{i}\|X_{i}\|_{\Psi_{2}}
.

Proof.

For any
s>0
, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(\max_{i}X_{i}>t)&=\mathbb{P}(\max_{i}\exp(sX_{i})>\exp st)\\
&\leq\exp(-st)\mathbb{E}[\max_{i}\exp(sX_{i})]\\
&\leq\exp(-st)\mathbb{E}[\sum_{i}^{n}\exp(sX_{i})]\\
&\leq n\exp(-st+s^{2}K^{2}).\end{split}

(66)

Take
s=t/K^{2}
and then it becomes
\mathbb{P}(\max_{i}X_{i}>t)\leq n\exp(-\frac{t^{2}}{2K^{2}})
.
Then consider
\mathbb{E}[\max_{i}X_{i}]
. For any
c>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[\max_{i}X_{i}]&=\frac{1}{c}\mathbb{E}[\log\exp(\max_{i}cX_{i})]\\
&\leq\frac{1}{c}\log\mathbb{E}[\exp(\max_{i}cX_{i})]\\
&=\frac{1}{c}\log\mathbb{E}[\max_{i}\exp cX_{i}]\\
&\leq\frac{1}{c}\log\mathbb{E}[\sum_{i}\exp cX_{i}]\\
&\leq\frac{\log n}{c}+\frac{cK^{2}}{2}.\end{split}

(67)

Take
c=\sqrt{2\log n}/K
and then get
\mathbb{E}[\max_{i}X_{i}]\leq K\sqrt{2\log n}
.
∎

Remark 3
.

When
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
are not centered, the upper bound of expectation term becomes

\mathbb{E}[\max_{i}X_{i}]\leq\max_{i}\mathbb{E}X_{i}+K\sqrt{2\log n}.

Appendix H
Proof of Tensor Recovery Empirical Process Theorem
11

Proof.

The proof follows from Theorem
14
. Recall that
{\mathbb{M}}_{{\mathbf{r}}}=\{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}\times\dots\times d_{m}}:\text{rank}({\mathbf{M}}_{(j)})\leq r_{j},\,j=1,\cdots,m\}
is the set of tensors with Tucker rank at most
{\mathbf{r}}
. Then consider

\displaystyle~~~~Z:=\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathbf{r}}}|f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*})-{\mathbb{E}}(f({\mathbf{M}})-f({\mathbf{M}}^{*}))|/\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}.

Together with
f({\mathbf{M}})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle-\xi_{i})
, we bound expectation of
Z

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}Z

\displaystyle\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathbf{r}}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}\cdot\big(\rho(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle-\xi_{i})-\rho(-\xi_{i})\big)\right|\cdot\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq 4\tilde{L}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathbf{r}}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}\cdot\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{i},{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\rangle\right|\cdot\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq 4\tilde{L}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|_{\mathrm{F,2{\mathbf{r}}}},

where
\{\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
is Rademacher sequence independent of
{\mathbf{X}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
. The first inequality is from Theorem
12
and the second inequality is from Theorem
13
. Notice that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}
has i.i.d. Gaussian entries and therefore

{\mathbb{E}}\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|_{\rm F,2{\mathbf{r}}}\leq C_{1}\sqrt{n(2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+2^{m}\cdot r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m})}
for some absolute constant
C_{1}>0
(see Lemma
23
). Thus we have

{\mathbb{E}}Z\leq 4C_{1}\tilde{L}\cdot\sqrt{n(2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+2^{m}\cdot r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m})}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle~~~~\|\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathbf{r}}}|\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}\rangle)-\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle)|\cdot\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}\|_{\Psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{L}\|\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathbf{r}}}|\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle|\cdot\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\tilde{L}\|\|{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\|_{\rm F,2{\mathbf{r}}}\|_{\Psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\tilde{L}\cdot\sqrt{2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+2^{m}\cdot r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m}},

for some absolute constant
C_{2}>0
.
Then, by Lemma
25
, the Orlicz norm of its maximum in
i=1,\cdots,n
could be bounded:

\displaystyle{~~~~~}\|\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathbf{r}}}|\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}\rangle)-\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle)|\cdot\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}\|_{\Psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\tilde{L}\sqrt{(2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+2^{m}\cdot r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m})\log n},

\displaystyle{~~~~~}\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\sup_{{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbb{M}}_{\mathbf{r}}}|\rho(Y_{ij}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{ij},\mathbf{M}\rangle)-\rho(Y_{ij}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{ij},\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle)|\cdot\|{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{M}}^{*}\|_{\rm{F}}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 3C_{2}\tilde{L}\sqrt{(2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+2^{m}\cdot r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m})\log n}.

Also, note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}\rangle)-\rho(Y_{i}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{M}^{*}\rangle)\big)^{2}\big]/\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{M}^{*}\|^{2}\leq\tilde{L}^{2}.

Invoke Theorem
14
and take
\eta=1
,
\delta=0.5
and there exists some constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq 8C_{2}\sqrt{n(2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+2^{m}\cdot r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m})}+t\right)

\displaystyle{~~~~~~~~~}\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{3n\tilde{L}^{2}}\right)+3\exp\left(-\left(\frac{t}{C\tilde{L}\sqrt{(2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+2^{m}\cdot r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m})\log n}}\right)^{2}\right)

holds for any
t>0
.
Take
t=C\tilde{L}\sqrt{n(2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+2^{m}\cdot r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m})}
and then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq(8C_{1}+C\tilde{L})\sqrt{n(2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+2^{m}\cdot r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m})}\right)

\displaystyle{~~~~~~~}\leq\exp\left(-\frac{C^{2}(2\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}d_{j}+2^{m}\cdot r_{1}r_{2}\cdots r_{m})}{3}\right)+3\exp\left(-\frac{n}{\log n}\right),

which completes the proof.
∎
